## topic group2 6-12 | Overview and data: International - treaties, agreements and developments in international organisations
### part 0: International Organisations – Headquarters, Heads, Full Forms and Roles
[0] h: Headquarters by city
[1] table: City | Organisations
  [1.0] Nairobi, Kenya | **UNEP** (1972), UN-Habitat
  [1.1] Rome, Italy | **FAO**, **IFAD**, WFP
  [1.2] Geneva | WHO, WTO, **ITU**, ILO, UNCTAD, WIPO, WMO
  [1.3] Bonn | UNFCCC secretariat (198 Parties), UNCCD
  [1.4] Vienna | IAEA, UNIDO, UNODC, OPEC
  [1.5] Paris | UNESCO, OECD, IEA
  [1.6] Montreal | ICAO
  [1.7] London | **IMO**, Commonwealth Secretariat
  [1.8] Washington D.C. | IMF, World Bank Group
  [1.9] Lyon, France | **INTERPOL**
  [1.10] Brussels, Belgium | **NATO**, European Commission
  [1.11] Monaco | **World Athletics** (formerly **IAAF**)
  [1.12] Amsterdam, Netherlands | **Greenpeace International** (NGO)
  [1.13] Shanghai / Beijing | New Development Bank / AIIB, SCO Secretariat
  [1.14] New York | UN, UNICEF, UNDP; UN Alliance of Civilizations (2005, set up by Türkiye + Spain)
  [1.15] New Delhi / Gurugram | CDRI / ISA secretariat
[2] x: **ITU** is in **Geneva**; **IMO** is the London agency; **IAAF** (World Athletics) is in **Monaco**.
[3] h: Full forms and roles
[4] table: Abbreviation | Full form and role
  [4.0] FAO | **Food and Agriculture Organization** (Rome; India a founding member)
  [4.1] INTERPOL | **International Criminal Police Organization** ("International Police Organisation"); est. 1923, HQ Lyon
  [4.2] UNEP | **Coordinates international environmental activities and policies**
  [4.3] IFAD | **International Fund for Agricultural Development** – outcome of the **1974 World Food Conference**; rural hunger and poverty in developing countries; HQ **Rome**; **43rd Governing Council**: **11-12 February 2020**, Rome
[5] h: Heads and nationalities
[6] table: Organisation | Head | Nationality
  [6.0] IMF | MD **Kristalina Georgieva** (from 1 Oct 2019; 2nd term from Oct 2024) | Bulgaria
  [6.1] World Bank | President **Ajay Banga** | U.S. (Indian-born)
  [6.2] WHO | DG Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus (from **2017**, the **first African** head) | Ethiopia
  [6.3] FAO | DG Qu Dongyu | China
  [6.4] WTO | DG Ngozi Okonjo-Iweala | Nigeria
  [6.5] ILO | DG Gilbert F. Houngbo | Togo
  [6.6] IAEA | DG Rafael Grossi | Argentina
  [6.7] WMO | SG Celeste Saulo | Argentina
  [6.8] IMO | SG Arsenio Dominguez | Panama
  [6.9] UNCTAD | SG Rebeca Grynspan | Costa Rica
  [6.10] UNESCO | DG Khaled El-Enany (from 15 Nov 2025) | Egypt
[7] b: **IMF:** **Christine Lagarde** was MD **2011-2019** (later **ECB** President); Georgieva succeeded her. **Jim Yong Kim** = former World Bank President; **Joseph Stiglitz** = former World Bank chief economist.
[8] b: **UNESCO:** El-Enany elected **6 Nov 2025** by the **43rd General Conference at Samarkand (Uzbekistan)** – **first Arab** head; succeeded Audrey Azoulay (France).
[9] b: Both the **WMO** and **IAEA** heads are from **Argentina**.
[10] h: Key facts of agencies
[11] table: Agency | Key detail
  [11.0] ITU (1865) | Oldest UN agency; **194 Member States** (193 UN members + Vatican City); India joined 1869; holds the World Telecommunication Development Conference every four years
  [11.1] UNESCO (1945) | 194 members + 12 associate members; **58-member** Executive Board
  [11.2] ICAO (1944) | Created by the Chicago Convention; 193 members; **36-member Council**; HQ Montreal
  [11.3] IMF | Bretton Woods, 1944; formal existence **27 Dec 1945** (29 members); operations from 1 Mar 1947
[12] b: **Bretton Woods Conference (1944):** based on the ideas of **Harry Dexter White** and **John Maynard Keynes**.
### part 1: Groupings and Organisations – Membership Changes and "Not a Member"
[0] h: Groupings and newest members
[1] table: Grouping | Members | Newest member or change
  [1.0] BRICS | – | Invited at Johannesburg, Aug 2023; joined **1 Jan 2024: Egypt, Ethiopia, Iran, UAE**; **Indonesia Jan 2025**
  [1.1] Eurozone | 21 | **Bulgaria 21st member, 1 Jan 2026** (replaced the lev); **Croatia** before it, 1 Jan 2023
  [1.2] SCO | Est. 15 June 2001 (Shanghai Five); HQ Beijing | **India and Pakistan full members 2017** (India an observer before)
  [1.3] Commonwealth | 56 | **Gabon (55th) and Togo (56th)** joined **25 June 2022**, CHOGM Kigali – no British colonial past; Rwanda 2009 before them
  [1.4] NATO | 32; HQ Brussels | Finland 31st (2023), Sweden 32nd (2024)
  [1.5] G20 | 19 countries + EU + African Union | AU joined 2023
  [1.6] Colombo Security Conclave | 6 | Seychelles 6th
  [1.7] APEC | 21 economies; Secretariat Singapore | India an observer, not a member
  [1.8] NDB | HQ Shanghai | Uzbekistan 10th member, first from Central Asia; Egypt joined 2023
[2] table: Fact | Key detail
  [2.0] BRICS invitations | **Argentina declined** its 2023 invitation; **Saudi Arabia** was invited but has **not formally confirmed** membership
  [2.1] Commonwealth suspensions | **Pakistan** suspended Nov 2007 (Musharraf's emergency), **readmitted 12 May 2008** (also suspended 1999-2004)
  [2.2] **Hungary** | EU member (2004) outside the euro area – currency **forint** (birr = Ethiopia, real = Brazil)
  [2.3] **Brexit referendum (23 June 2016)** | **Leave 51.9% : Remain 48.1% (≈ 52 : 48)**; the UK left the EU on **31 Jan 2020**
  [2.4] **League of Nations (1920)** | The **USA never joined** – the Senate did not ratify the Treaty of Versailles; China, British India and Cuba were members
  [2.5] **International Solar Alliance** | **Malta** became its **119th member**
  [2.6] **APEC 2025 summit** | **Gyeongju, South Korea**; China proposed **WAICO** for AI governance
[3] x: The newest **Eurozone** member is **Bulgaria** (2026; Croatia 2023); **India** joined the **SCO** as a full member in **2017**.

[5] b: **Manipuri Sankirtana:** Meitei **song-and-dance** performance narrating the **life and deeds of Krishna**, performed by **two drummers (pung)** and about ten singer-dancers.

  [7.2] **Makli necropolis** | Near **Thatta (Sindh, Pakistan)**; UNESCO World Heritage Site (**1981**)
### part 3: India's Bilateral Agreements – MoUs, Tax Treaties and Mobility Pacts
[0] table: Agreement | Partner | Date and content

  [0.3] Comprehensive Migration and Mobility Partnership | Germany | 5 December 2022
[1] b: **Mobility pacts:** **France (2018)** first; India later signed similar pacts with the UK (2021), Germany (2022) and Italy (2023).
### part 4: INTERPOL, Initiatives, Forums and Scientific Bodies
[0] h: INTERPOL colour-coded notices
[1] table: Notice | Purpose
  [1.0] Red | Locate and arrest a wanted person pending extradition
  [1.1] Blue | **Collect additional information** on a person's identity, location or activities
  [1.2] Yellow | Missing persons
  [1.3] Black | Information on **unidentified bodies**
  [1.4] Green | **Warning** about a person's criminal activities (threat to public safety)
  [1.5] Silver (newest, Jan 2025, pilot) | **Identify and trace criminal assets**
[2] b: In India the **CBI** is the INTERPOL **National Central Bureau**; a Blue Notice was issued for the Goa nightclub owners after a fire killed **25**.
[3] b: **INTERPOL Operation Storm Makers II (2023):** **Telangana** police registered one of India's first cases of **human trafficking to force victims into cyber fraud** (an accountant lured to Southeast Asia, freed after ransom).
[4] h: Initiatives and bodies – sponsor and secretariat
[5] table: Initiative | Sponsor | Secretariat or objective
  [5.0] CDRI | India (2019) | HQ New Delhi
  [5.1] IPCC | UNEP + WMO | AR7 cycle from July 2023
  [5.2] POWERR Asia | Japan, under AZEC Plus, Apr 2026 | Energy resilience
  [5.3] ITPGRFA ("Seed Treaty") | FAO | Plant genetic resources for food and agriculture
  [5.4] Great Reset (2020) | World Economic Forum | WEF initiative
  [5.5] Colombo Process | Asian labour-sending countries | Regional forum on overseas employment
  [5.6] Pax Silica | U.S.-led | Critical minerals, semiconductor and AI supply chains; India joined Feb 2026
  [5.7] Trade and Technology Council | **U.S. + EU** | Launched **June 2021** (first met Pittsburgh, Sep 2021) to **coordinate trade and technology policy**
[6] x: **Colombo Process** = labour migration; **Colombo Security Conclave (2020)** = India, Sri Lanka, Maldives, Mauritius, Bangladesh + **Seychelles (6th)**.
[7] b: India's leadership roles: **Kimberley Process chair 2026**, host of **ITU PP-30**, founder of **ISA, CDRI** and the **Global Biofuels Alliance** (launched under India's G20, 2023).

### part 7: International Conventions, Treaties and India's Status
[0] h: Conventions and their subjects
[1] table: Convention or framework | Year | Governs | Key detail
  [1.0] Chicago | 1944 | Civil aviation | Created ICAO
  [1.1] Hague Conventions (HCCH) | – | Private international law | Apostille (1961), Child Abduction (1980)
  [1.2] Vienna | 1985 | Ozone layer protection | –
  [1.3] Montreal Protocol | 1987 | Ozone-depleting substances | Universally ratified (197)
  [1.4] Basel | 1989 | Transboundary hazardous waste | –
  [1.5] Water Convention (Helsinki) | 1992 | Transboundary rivers and lakes | Bangladesh first South Asian party
  [1.6] Rotterdam | – | Prior informed consent for hazardous chemicals | –
  [1.7] Stockholm | 2001 | Persistent organic pollutants (POPs) | 34 chemicals
  [1.8] Minamata | 2013 | Mercury | Dental amalgam phased out by 2034
  [1.9] Windsor Framework | 2023 | UK-EU post-Brexit trade rules for Northern Ireland | –
  [1.10] UN Convention against Cybercrime | Dec 2024 | Cybercrime | First global cybercrime treaty; **India signed it on 25 Sep 2026**
[2] table: Fact | Key detail
  [2.0] **Kigali Amendment** | India to stop clearances for **new HFC production beyond 31 Dec 2027**
  [2.1] **Alaska** | Bought by the **USA from Russia in 1867** (Treaty of Cession, Seward); 49th U.S. state 1959
  [2.2] **EU Constitution** | Drafted under **Valery Giscard d'Estaing**; failed in **2005** referendums; replaced by the Lisbon Treaty (2009)
[3] h: Recent treaties and India's status
[4] table: Treaty | Subject or key date | India's status
  [4.0] BBNJ / High Seas Treaty (under UNCLOS) | In force **17 Jan 2026** | Signed, not ratified
  [4.1] UN outer-space treaties (five) | – | Signed all five, ratified four; not the Moon Agreement (1979)
  [4.2] Washington Accords | U.S.-brokered DR Congo-Rwanda peace, 4 Dec 2025 | –
[5] b: **Treaty-making in India:** concluded **in the name of the President**; **Article 253** lets Parliament legislate to implement treaties.
### part 8: Trade Agreements and the WTO
[0] h: India's trade agreements and partners
[1] table: Agreement | Partner | Key detail
  [1.0] CEPA (2022) | UAE | –
  [1.1] ECTA | Australia | –
  [1.2] TEPA | EFTA | –
  [1.3] CETA | UK | "Gold standard" deal
  [1.4] CEPA | Oman | In force Jun 2026; 99% of India's exports duty-free; India's second GCC CEPA
  [1.5] FTA | EU | "Mother of All Deals"; with an Investment Protection Agreement and a GI Agreement
[2] h: GATT to WTO
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] GATT | Signed 1947; in force 1948
  [3.1] Uruguay Round | 8th GATT round, from 1986; concluded **15 Dec 1993**; Marrakesh Agreement **15 Apr 1994**; WTO from **1 Jan 1995**
  [3.2] Dunkel Draft (1991) | Proposals of GATT DG Arthur Dunkel during the Uruguay Round
  [3.3] TRIMs | Trade-Related Investment Measures
[4] b: **Criticisms:** Appellate Body paralysed; voting imbalance in the Bretton Woods institutions (quota-based voting not abolished); MC14 failure + U.S. exits → **weakening multilateralism**.
### part 9: IMF, World Bank Group and Multilateral Banks
[0] h: IMF
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] **France** | **First borrower** from the IMF
  [1.1] Lending | Mainly for **balance-of-payments** problems
  [1.2] **Rapid Financing Instrument** | Quick liquidity **without a full programme** (disasters, pandemics, post-conflict)
  [1.3] **Structural Adjustment Programmes (1980s-90s)** | Austerity, privatisation, devaluation
  [1.4] **BRICS** | About **20% of world GDP** but 11% of IMF votes in **2011**
[2] h: World Bank Group and other banks
[3] table: Body | Key detail
  [3.0] IFC | Private-sector arm of the World Bank Group
  [3.1] IDA (1960) | Zero- or low-interest loans and grants to the poorest countries
  [3.2] New Development Bank | 2014, Fortaleza Declaration; HQ Shanghai; founders' vote cannot fall below 55%; **no veto** for any country
  [3.3] AIIB | Est. 2016; 111 approved members; HQ Beijing
  [3.4] BRICS Contingency Reserve Arrangement | IMF-type liquidity support for BRICS members
[4] h: Multilateral lending and projects
[5] table: Lender | Project
  [5.0] World Bank + ADB + HUDCO | Amaravati – about ₹26,000 crore facilitated by the Centre
  [5.1] World Bank | SALT road and school projects, Andhra Pradesh
  [5.2] AIIB + World Bank | Mission Schools of Excellence, Gujarat
  [5.3] NDB with ADB | Delhi-Ghaziabad-Meerut RRTS
  [5.4] World Bank | Bangladesh's Gazipur-Elenga (N4) and Natore-Nawabganj (N6) highways

### part 1: Defence Exercises (Oct–Nov 2026)
[0] h: Exercises of the period at a glance
[1] table: Exercise | Partner | Service | Venue and edition
  [1.0] **Konkan** | UK | Navy | Plymouth; since **2004**
  [1.1] **Mountain Guardian** | UAE | Army | Chaubatia, Uttarakhand
  [1.2] **INDRA** | Russia | Navy | Vladivostok, Sea of Japan; since **2003**
  [1.3] **AUSTRAHIND** | Australia | Army | Thiruvananthapuram; 5th
  [1.4] **SIMBEX** | Singapore | Navy | Visakhapatnam; 33rd; since **1994**
  [1.5] **Garuda Shakti** | Indonesia | Special Forces | Banyuwangi, East Java; 11th
  [1.6] **IN-RoKN** | South Korea | Navy | Busan
  [1.7] **BIMSTEC Maritime Security Exercise** | BIMSTEC (7) | Navy | Vijayapuram, A&N; 1st
  [1.8] **Tiger Triumph** | US | Tri-service (HADR) | Visakhapatnam; 5th
  [1.9] **Akhand Prahar-II** | Indian Army only | Army | Jaisalmer
  [1.10] **Keen Sword 27** | US-Japan (India not a party) | Multi-domain | Southwestern Japan
[2] h: Details (Oct–Nov 2026)
[3] table: Exercise | Dates and venue | Key detail
  [3.0] **Konkan-2026** (India-UK, news 15 Oct) | **8-15 October**, off **Plymouth** (in **Devon**, home to **HM Naval Base Devonport**) | Annual exercise of the **Indian Navy** and **UK Royal Navy**; **Talwar-class** guided-missile stealth frigate **INS Trishul** (**F43**, commissioned **25 June 2003**; Talwar class initially **built in Russia**); **Harbour Phase** and **Sea Phase** (air, surface, underwater warfare, helicopter cross-deck landings, firing drills, anti-submarine warfare)
  [3.1] Konkan context | – | Part of the **India-UK Comprehensive Strategic Partnership** and **India-UK Vision 2035**; other India-UK exercises **Ajeya Warrior (Army)** and **Indradhanush (Air Force)**; UK capital London, currency Pound Sterling; Indian Navy HQ New Delhi, **Navy Day 4 December**
  [3.2] **Mountain Guardian-2026** (India-UAE, news 18 Oct) | **5-18 October 2026**, **Chaubatia Foreign Training Centre, Uttarakhand** | **Mountain warfare**; **92 personnel, 46 from each side**; Indian **Bhairav Battalion** and **UAE Mountain Infantry Battalion**; **women officers and female combat personnel** took part
  [3.3] **INDRA-2026** (India-Russia, news 19 Oct) | **13-18 October**, **Vladivostok** (Harbour Phase) and **Sea of Japan** (Sea Phase) | **Shivalik-class** stealth frigate **INS Sahyadri** (**F49**, commissioned **21 July 2012**; Shivalik class built by **Mazagon Dock Shipbuilders, Mumbai**); Russian corvette **Hero of the Russian Federation Aldar Tsydenzhapov**; Vladivostok = HQ of the **Russian Pacific Fleet**; Russia capital Moscow, currency **Ruble**
  [3.4] **AUSTRAHIND-2026** (5th India-Australia, news 21 Oct) | **19 October – 1 November 2026**, **Foreign Training Node, Thiruvananthapuram**, Kerala | Theme **Joint Sub-Conventional Operations in Island and Coastal Environment** under **Chapter VII of the UN Mandate**; annual **Army** exercise launched **2022**; 4th edition **13-26 October 2025**, **Irwin Barracks, Perth**; other exercises **AUSINDEX (naval)**, **Pitch Black** (multinational air exercise hosted by Australia); Australia capital **Canberra**
  [3.5] **Akhand Prahar-II** (news 22 Oct) | **Jaisalmer, Rajasthan** | Follows Akhand Prahar-I; by the **Black Mace Brigade** and **Battle Axe Division** (under **Southern Command, HQ Pune**); tested tactics, coordination, next-generation equipment and force multipliers; **Indian Army Day 15 January** (**K. M. Cariappa** became first Indian Commander-in-Chief in **1949**)
  [3.6] **SIMBEX-2026** (33rd India-Singapore, news 23 Oct) | **Visakhapatnam** (AP), under **Eastern Naval Command (HQ Visakhapatnam)** | Opening onboard **INS Nilgiri** (**Project 17A** stealth frigate) and **INS Kamorta** (**Project 28** ASW corvette); India's **longest-running uninterrupted bilateral naval exercise**; started **1994** as **Exercise Lion King**; Singapore currency **Singapore Dollar**
  [3.7] **Keen Sword 27** (US-Japan, news 27 Oct) | Islands and waters of **southwestern Japan** | **Largest joint field-level exercise** in their history; **biennial** multi-domain **Field Training Exercise** of the **Japan Self-Defense Forces** and **US Armed Forces**; **US-Japan Treaty of Mutual Cooperation and Security** signed **1960**
  [3.8] **Garuda Shakti 2026** (11th India-Indonesia Special Forces, news 28 Oct) | **27 October – 6 November 2026**, **Banyuwangi, East Java** | India: **Parachute Regiment (Special Forces)**; jungle operations, obstacle crossing, precision targeting, mountaineering, airborne assaults; **Samudra Shakti** = India-Indonesia **naval** exercise; Indonesia capital Jakarta, currency Rupiah
  [3.9] **IN-RoKN** (India-South Korea, news 29 Oct) | INS Sahyadri reached **Busan Naval Port on 26 Oct 2026** | Maritime cooperation and interoperability in the Indo-Pacific; INS Sahyadri built under **Project 17**; ties upgraded to **Special Strategic Partnership in 2015**; South Korea capital **Seoul**, currency **Won**
  [3.10] **First BIMSTEC Maritime Security Exercise** (news 1 Nov) | **30 Oct – 3 Nov 2026**, hosted by India at **Vijayapuram, Andaman and Nicobar Islands** | **All seven members**: **Bangladesh, Bhutan, India, Myanmar, Nepal, Sri Lanka, Thailand**; objective "**Translating Vision to Action**"; naval interoperability, maritime domain awareness, coordination in the **Bay of Bengal**
  [3.11] BIMSTEC context | **6th BIMSTEC Summit**, **4 Apr 2025, Bangkok** – theme "**BIMSTEC: Prosperous, Resilient and Open**" | BIMSTEC est. **6 Jun 1997** by the **Bangkok Declaration**; HQ **Dhaka**; Secretary-General **Indra Mani Pandey (India)**
  [3.12] **9th India-Sri Lanka Coast Guard High-Level Meeting** (news 4 Nov) | **Mirissa**, Sri Lanka | Led by **ICG DG Paramesh Sivamani** and Sri Lanka's **DG Ruwan Rupasena**; marine pollution response, maritime SAR, law enforcement; **ICG est. 1 Feb 1977**, formally inaugurated **18 Aug 1978**, HQ New Delhi, under **MoD**; **Coast Guard Day 1 February**; India and Sri Lanka separated by the **Palk Strait** and **Gulf of Mannar**
  [3.13] **"Lokayan-26"** voyage of **INS Sudarshini** (news 3 Nov) | From Kochi on **20 Jan 2026**; returned to **Kochi on 2 Nov 2026** (10 months) | Sail training ship; **22,000+ nautical miles** across Asia, Europe, Africa and North America; **17 port calls in 11 countries**; commissioned **27 Jan 2012**, built by **Goa Shipyard Ltd**; **Southern Naval Command (HQ Kochi)** = the Navy's **Training Command**; **1 nautical mile = 1.852 km**

[0] h: Lindsey O. Graham Sanctioning Russia and Iran Act, 2026
[1] table: Fact | Key detail

  [1.1] Tariff | Up to 100% on imports from countries buying large amounts of Russian crude/gas; in addition to other duties
  [1.2] Nature | Discretionary – the tariff is not automatic
  [1.3] Applies to | After 30 days, the top 5 importers of Russian energy (last 12 months) or the top 5 sanction-evasion facilitators

  [1.5] Presidential waiver | Needs written national-interest certification + report to Congress
  [1.6] Review | USTR review within 180 days

### part 8: Strait of Hormuz and the US–Iran Conflict
[0] h: The 20% transit fee
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Proposal | President **Donald Trump**: a **20% reimbursement fee** on cargo through the Strait of Hormuz ("**Guardian of the Hormuz Strait**")
  [1.1] Fate | **Withdrawn one day later**; replaced by **trade and investment deals with Gulf countries**
  [1.2] **IMO** | Opposed – **no legal basis for tolls** on international strait transit
  [1.3] **Iran's FM** | Called 20% excessive
[2] x: **Neither the US nor Iran has ratified UNCLOS**, but transit passage is **customary law**; tolls apply only to **artificial canals (Suez, Panama)**.
[3] h: US–Iran context
[4] table: Date | Event
  [4.0] **February 2026** | Conflict began; Iran asserted sovereignty and **temporarily closed the Strait**
  [4.1] **17 June 2026** | Interim pact: **US–Iran 14-point MoU** – **permanent ceasefire**, **phased end to the US naval blockade**, **reopening of the Strait of Hormuz**
  [4.2] **24 June** | Transits crossed **90**; average **40–50/day** vs pre-war up to **140**
  [4.3] **July** | Ceasefire **collapsed** (Trump called it "over", **7 Jul**)
  [4.4] **August** | The **60-day window** for a final deal lapsed without extension
[5] h: India's exposure
[6] table: Import | Share via Hormuz
  [6.0] Crude oil | About **40%**
  [6.1] LNG | About **60%**
  [6.2] LPG | About **90%**
[7] table: Fact | Key detail
  [7.0] Import dependence | **Crude 88%+**; LPG **60%**; natural gas about **50%**
  [7.1] Volume | **1.8–2 billion barrels/yr**; every **USD 1/barrel** adds up to **USD 2 billion**
  [7.2] Cost of the fee | About **USD 9 billion/yr**
  [7.3] Oil imports **March–May 2026** | Up **47%** to **USD 48.88 billion**

[2] h: India's nuclear fuel picture
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] Capacity target | **9 GW → 100 GW by 2047**
  [3.1] Uranium import dependence | **>70%**; domestic supply max **30%** of future needs
  [3.2] Suppliers | Uzbekistan, Kazakhstan, Canada, Russia (Russia – lifetime supply for **Kudankulam**)
  [3.3] Ore grade | Domestic **0.02–0.45%** vs global **1–2%**; some Canadian mines up to **15%**
  [3.4] Exploration | **15** states; **Prototype Fast Breeder Reactor** – **Kalpakkam**
  [3.5] First mine | **Jaduguda, Jharkhand (1967)**
  [3.6] Reserves | **Tummalapalle (Andhra Pradesh)** – one of the world's largest; **Narwapahar, Bhatin** (Jharkhand); **Domiasiat** (Meghalaya); **Rohil** (Rajasthan)
  [3.7] Agency / need | **UCIL** under DAE; **1,500–2,000 t/year** (**1,884 t** in 2025)
  [3.8] **CIRUS** (Canada India Reactor Utility Service) | India's **second reactor, 1950s**; **RAPP (Rajasthan)** in the 1960s
  [3.9] Uranium | Atomic number **92**, mass ~238 u; ore **Uraninite (Pitchblende)**; **U-238 ~99.3% (fertile)**, **U-235 ~0.7% (fissile)**
  [3.10] Reactors | Power reactors use **3–5% enriched LEU**; the **three-stage programme (Homi Bhabha)** ultimately aims at **thorium**
[4] x: **Tummalapalle**, one of the world's largest uranium reserves, is in **Andhra Pradesh**.

### part 4: Strait of Hormuz Crisis and UNCLOS
[0] h: Iran's selective blockade and toll system
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Blockade | Iran **closed** the strait for the **US, Israel and allies**; **open** to "friendly nations" **India, China, Russia, Iraq, Pakistan** (FM **Araghchi**)
  [1.1] Traffic | **~130 ships/day → 3–4/day**; **20+ vessels attacked**; **~2,000 stranded**
  [1.2] **Toll-booth system** | Geopolitical vetting, clearance code, escort; **up to $2 million per ship**, some in **yuan** (Iran denies)
  [1.3] Indian ships through Hormuz | **Pine Gas, Jag Vasant, Shivalik, Nanda Devi, Jag Laadki** – India had **no formal agreement and made no payments**
  [1.4] **GCC** | Called the tolls a clear violation of law
  [1.5] Workarounds | US **Project Freedom** escorts; **Habshan–Fujairah pipeline** (UAE) **bypasses** the strait
  [1.6] **Philippines** | President **Ferdinand Marcos Jr.** declared a **National Energy Emergency** – imports **~98%** of its oil
[2] h: UNCLOS – straits articles
[3] table: Article | Provision
  [3.0] **38** | Right of transit passage
  [3.1] **39** | Duties of ships during passage
  [3.2] **44** | Coastal States cannot suspend or hamper passage
[4] h: Geography of Hormuz
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] Location | Between **Iran (north)** and **Oman (Musandam) & UAE (south)**
  [5.1] Links | **Persian Gulf** with the **Gulf of Oman / Arabian Sea**; **no direct link to the Red Sea**
  [5.2] Width | **~50 km at entry**, **~33 km at narrowest**
  [5.3] Flows | **~20 mb/d** (**~20%** of global oil consumption, about one-fifth of petroleum); **20–30% of global LNG** (incl. **Qatar LNG**); trade **~$600 billion a year**
  [5.4] Users | **Iran, Iraq, Kuwait, Qatar, Saudi Arabia, UAE**; the Gulf produces **22%** of global oil
[6] h: Other chokepoints
[7] table: Waterway | Location / link
  [7.0] **Bab-el-Mandeb** | Between Yemen and Djibouti/Eritrea; Red Sea ↔ Gulf of Aden
  [7.1] Linked to the Red Sea | Gulf of Aden, Suez Canal, Bab-el-Mandeb
  [7.2] **Strait of Hormuz** | Persian Gulf ↔ Gulf of Oman
[8] h: IRIS Dena and UNCLOS zones
[9] table: Fact | Key detail
  [9.0] **IRIS Dena** | Iranian Navy frigate returning from the **International Fleet Review (IFR) 2026, Visakhapatnam**; reportedly sunk by the **US** (83 killed), March 2026
  [9.1] Where | **International waters south of Sri Lanka near Galle** – **outside** Sri Lanka's **12 nm territorial sea** but **within its Search and Rescue (SAR) region**
  [9.2] Rescue | **Sri Lankan Navy** rescued survivors under UNCLOS
  [9.3] UNCLOS | "**Constitution of the Oceans**"; adopted **10 December 1982** at **Montego Bay, Jamaica**; in force **16 November 1994**
  [9.4] Parties | **170+** (168 UN members + EU); **India is a party; the USA has not ratified**
[10] table: Zone / body | Extent / seat
  [10.0] Territorial Sea | **12 nm**
  [10.1] Contiguous Zone | **24 nm**
  [10.2] EEZ | **200 nm**
  [10.3] **ITLOS** | Hamburg
  [10.4] **International Seabed Authority** | Kingston
  [10.5] **CLCS** | New York
[11] h: UN Task Force on Hormuz Trade
[12] b: Set up by **António Guterres**; led by **Jorge Moreira da Silva** (Executive Director, **UNOPS**); **UNCTAD, IMO, ICC** take part; political coordination **Jean Arnault**.
[13] table: Body | Founded – HQ
  [13.0] **UNOPS** | **1973** – Copenhagen
  [13.1] **UNCTAD** | **1964** – Geneva
  [13.2] **IMO** | Convention **1948**, working from **1959** – London
  [13.3] **ICC** (International Chamber of Commerce) | **1919** – Paris
### part 5: US–Israel–Iran War (2026)
[0] h: Operations – who did what
[1] table: Operation | By | Key detail
  [1.0] **Epic Fury** | **United States** | Against Iran, from 28 Feb 2026; name given officially by the US Department of Defense, coordinated with Israel; cost $33.4 bn up to 30 Jun 2026
  [1.1] **Lion's Roar** | **Israel** | Coordinated strikes on Iran
  [1.2] **True Promise 4** | **Iran** | Retaliation – missiles on Israel and Gulf states
  [1.3] **Project Freedom** | US Navy / US Central Command (CENTCOM) | May 2026 – escorts to protect commercial shipping in Hormuz; 100+ aircraft and drones, ~15,000 personnel
  [1.4] **Ghazab Lil Haq** | Pakistan | Operation in news
  [1.5] **Midnight Hammer** | US | June 2025 – strikes on Natanz, Isfahan; US B-2 bunker-busters on Fordow
[2] h: Iran – leadership and events
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] **Supreme Leader Ayatollah Ali Khamenei** | Killed (assassinated) in the US–Israel strike on **28 Feb 2026**; aim described as **decapitation / regime change**
  [3.1] Successor | **Mojtaba Khamenei** (**March 2026**)
  [3.2] Government | President **Masoud Pezeshkian**; Foreign Minister **Seyyed Abbas Araghchi**
  [3.3] **Chabahar** | Hit by strikes (**18 Jul 2026**); India's **Shahid Beheshti terminal undamaged**
  [3.4] Background | **1979 Iranian Revolution** – ties with Israel severed; US = "**Great Satan**", Israel = "**Little Satan**"
  [3.5] **Sejjil (Ashura)** | **Two-stage solid-fuel medium-range ballistic missile**, first launched against Israeli and US bases; range **2,500 km**; up to **Mach 13**; the **"dancing missile"** – manoeuvres at high altitude to evade air defences
[4] h: India's stakes
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] Crude | India = **third-largest crude consumer**; imports **85–88%** of crude; **2.5–2.7 mb/d** via Hormuz (from **Iraq, Saudi Arabia, UAE, Kuwait**)
  [5.1] Reserves | Strategic reserves **only for crude** (none large for LPG/LNG)
  [5.2] Diaspora | **~9 million Indians** in West Asia; past evacuations **Operation Rahat**, **Operation Ajay**
  [5.3] Connectivity at risk | **Chabahar Port** and **IMEC**
[6] h: Abraham Accords
[7] table: Fact | Key detail
  [7.0] Nature | **US-brokered** normalisation between **Israel** and Muslim-majority countries; launched **2020**
  [7.1] First signatories (2020) | **UAE, Bahrain, Morocco**
  [7.2] Later | **Kosovo, Sudan, Somaliland, Kazakhstan**
  [7.3] In talks only | **Saudi Arabia, Qatar, Pakistan, Türkiye, Egypt, Jordan**
  [7.4] Israel | Capital **Jerusalem**; currency **New Shekel**; PM **Benjamin Netanyahu**
[8] h: UN blacklist on conflict-related sexual violence
[9] table: Fact | Key detail
  [9.0] Report | **UN Secretary-General's 2026 report** blacklisted **Russian and Israeli armed forces for the first time**; both objected; published for **15 years**
  [9.1] UN | Est. **1945**; HQ **New York**; SG **António Guterres**

### part 7: Sanctions, CAATSA, Chabahar and Indian Ocean Ports
[0] h: Sanctions
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] **Unilateral sanctions** | Imposed **without UNSC approval**
  [1.1] **Secondary/extraterritorial** sanctions | Target **third countries**
  [1.2] **CAATSA** | Countering America's Adversaries Through Sanctions Act, **2017** – targets **Russia, Iran, North Korea**
  [1.3] **S-400 Triumf** | **$5.4 billion** deal with **Russia (2018)**; the **US did not sanction India**
[2] b: Under US pressure India stopped **Iranian oil after 2019** sanctions, cut **Venezuelan** oil and reduced **Russian** oil purchases (late 2025–early 2026); the US waiver for Chabahar is nearing expiry.
[3] h: Chabahar, Gwadar and INSTC
[4] table: Fact | Key detail
  [4.0] **Chabahar** | South-eastern Iran on the **Gulf of Oman** – outside the Persian Gulf; Iran's only oceanic port
  [4.1] Rival project | **Gwadar** (Pakistan)
  [4.2] **Shahid Beheshti terminal** | Run by **India Ports Global Limited (IPGL)**; **10-year** agreement (May 2024)
  [4.3] **INSTC** | **7,200 km** multimodal – India, Iran, Russia, Central Asia, Europe
### part 8: 1973 and 1979 Oil Crises – Comparison with 2026
[0] h: 1973 oil crisis – the chain
[1] b: **Yom Kippur War (1973)** → **Arab members of OPEC (OAPEC)** imposed an **oil embargo** on countries **supporting Israel** → prices rose **~fourfold (~400%, 1973–74)** → **stagflation**.
[2] table: Fact | Key detail
  [2.0] Other factors | **End of the gold standard (1971)**; territories lost in the **1967 war**
  [2.1] Outcomes | **Petrodollar system**, **strategic petroleum reserves (SPRs)**, creation of the **International Energy Agency (IEA)**
  [2.2] Significance | **First major use of oil as a geopolitical weapon**
[3] h: Comparison
[4] table: Crisis | Cause | India
  [4.0] **1973** | Political embargo (**OAPEC**) | Prices **$3 → $5+** (~70% initial rise); oil import bill **$500 million (1973) → $1.3 billion (1974)**; inflation ~**20%**; **Fifth Five-Year Plan** assumptions upset
  [4.1] **1979** | **Iranian Revolution**; also stagflation | Prices **$13 (1979) → ~$34 (1980)**; **BoP crisis** → India approached the **IMF** after 1979
  [4.2] **2026** | **Physical disruption** of supply routes – **Strait of Hormuz** | Selective passage; no payments
[5] x: India went to the **IMF after 1979**. 1973 = political embargo; 2026 = physical disruption.
[6] h: India's energy basket today
[7] table: Fact | Key detail
  [7.0] Energy demand (IEA) | **Coal ~48.4%**, **crude ~24.7%**
  [7.1] Domestic crude | Meets **~13%**; import dependence **~88.5% (FY26)**
  [7.2] **Strategic Petroleum Reserves** | **~5.33 million tonnes** capacity

### part 10: Quick Revision and Traps
[0] h: Numbers
[1] table: Item | Key detail
  [1.0] Hormuz traffic | **~130 → 3–4 ships/day**
  [1.1] Hormuz toll | Up to **$2 million per ship**
  [1.2] Hormuz narrowest / entry | **~33 km** / **~50 km**
  [1.3] UNCLOS straits articles | **38, 39, 44**

### part 2: India–US Trade Deal (February 2026)
[0] h: Tariff cut
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] **Reciprocal tariff** | **25% → 18%**
  [1.1] Punitive duty | The **additional 25%** (imposed **August 2025** over **Russian oil** purchases) **removed**
  [1.2] Effective burden | **~50% → 18%** – lower than **Vietnam/Bangladesh (~20%)** and **China (30–35%)**
  [1.3] India's expected commitments | Cut Russian crude, buy from the US (and possibly **Venezuela**); lower tariffs / NTBs; purchases **up to USD 500 billion** of US energy, agriculture, coal, technology
  [1.4] Trump's claim | India "agreed to **stop buying Russian oil**" (**Donald Trump**)
  [1.5] **GSP** | Still lost (withdrawn in **2019**); the US tariff is now **18%**, above zero
[2] h: Sector-wise tariffs
[3] table: Sector | Tariff | US market
  [3.0] Textiles & apparel | **50% → 18%**; silk 0% | **USD 113 bn**
  [3.1] Leather & footwear | **50% → 18%** | **USD 42 bn**
  [3.2] Gems & jewellery | 18%; 0% diamonds, platinum, coins | **USD 29 bn** (0% segment)
  [3.3] Home décor | 18%; 0% seats, chandeliers | **USD 52 bn**
  [3.4] Toys | 18% | **USD 18 bn**
  [3.5] Machinery | 18% | **USD 477 bn** (India exports **USD 2.35 bn**)
[4] h: Bilateral Trade Agreement – numbers
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] What India gets | **18%** rate on **USD 900 bn** of US imports; **zero duty on USD 150 bn**; no additional duty on **USD 720 bn**; continued exemption on **USD 350 bn**; preferential treatment on **Section 232** tariffs
  [5.1] Reciprocal relief | Exports of **USD 40.96 bn** faced 50% – **USD 30.94 bn cut to 18%**, **USD 10.03 bn cut to zero**
  [5.2] **Section 232** (national-security tariffs) | Zero reciprocal duty for **USD 28.30 bn** of exports
  [5.3] **Zero-duty industrial exports** | **USD 38 bn** – aircraft parts, generic drugs, elementary auto parts, diamonds, platinum
[6] h: Agriculture
[7] table: Fact | Key detail
  [7.0] Agri surplus with the US | **USD 1.3 bn** (exports **3.4 bn**, imports **2.1 bn**)
  [7.1] Zero additional duty | **USD 1.36 bn** agri exports – **spices, tea, coffee, cashew, mango, fruit pulp**; **USD 1.035 bn** assured zero reciprocal tariff
  [7.2] **Fully protected** | **Dairy, poultry, meat, GM foods, millets, maize, cereals, pulses, oilseeds, honey, ethanol, tobacco**
  [7.3] Tools | Phased elimination up to **10 years**; **Tariff Rate Quotas** (quantity-limited)
[8] h: Background
[9] table: Fact | Key detail
  [9.0] Pre-deal steps | Duty cuts on **heavy motorcycles, bourbon whisky**; **SHANTI Act, 2025** (Sustainable Harnessing and Advancement of Nuclear Energy for Transforming India) – **opened the nuclear sector to private players**
  [9.1] Bilateral trade | **FY25: USD 132 bn** (FY24 USD 119.71 bn); India's surplus **USD 40.82 bn**; the US is **India's largest trading partner** (~USD 128–132 bn)
  [9.2] Exports under 50% tariffs | Fell **12% (September 2025)** and **8.5% (October 2025)**
  [9.3] Investment | US = **3rd largest investor** in India – **USD 70.65 bn (2000–2025)**
  [9.4] **US–India COMPACT (2025)** | Catalyzing Opportunities for Military Partnership, Accelerated Commerce & Technology; technology cooperation under **iCET**
  [9.5] **Mission 500** | Bilateral trade **USD 500 bn by 2030**; **BTA** under negotiation
  [9.6] Energy import dependence | **88.2% (FY25)**
  [9.7] Digital trade | Global digitally delivered services **USD 4.78 trillion (2024)**; India's digital exports **USD 0.28 trillion (+10.3%)**; India **5th in exports, 11th in imports**
### part 3: India–New Zealand FTA and Trade Agreement Types
[0] h: The FTA
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Concluded | **December 2025**, in a **record nine months**; negotiated by a **women-led team**
  [1.1] First | **India's first FTA with New Zealand**
[2] table: Side | Commitment
  [2.0] New Zealand | Zero duty on **100%** of Indian exports
  [2.1] India | Tariff relief on **95%** of NZ imports; **57%** duty-free from day one
  [2.2] Investment | NZ commits **$20 billion** over **15 years**, with a clawback provision
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] **Excluded by India** | **Dairy** (milk, cheese, butter, yogurt), **onions, sugar, edible oils, spices, rubber**
  [3.1] Agri cooperation (included) | **Kiwifruit, apples, honey**
  [3.2] Trade | **$1.3 bn**, to double in five years
  [3.3] NZ | Per capita income ~**$49,380**; Indian diaspora ~**5%** of the population (~3 lakh)
  [3.4] Criticism | A coalition partner called it "**neither free nor fair**" – its Foreign Minister to vote against it in 2026
[4] h: Types of trade agreements (ascending integration)
[5] table: Type | Feature
  [5.0] **Partial Scope Agreement** | Limited goods
  [5.1] **FTA** | Tariffs cut among members; own tariffs for others
  [5.2] **Customs Union** | + common external tariff
  [5.3] **Common Market** | + free movement of capital and labour
  [5.4] **Economic Union** | + macroeconomic coordination
[6] b: ASEAN FTA partners include **Australia, China, India, Japan**.

### part 10: High-probability MCQ Traps

### part 1: Iran – US Strikes, 12-Day War and Snapback
[0] h: US strikes on nuclear sites (June 2025)
[1] b: The US joined the **Israel–Iran conflict** (reignited June 2025).
[2] table: Site | Key detail | Weapon

  [2.1] **Fordow** | The underground site – under a mountain near **Qom**, ~**100 km** SW of Tehran; exposed **2009** | **12 GBU-57 MOPs** from six **B-2** bombers
  [2.2] **Isfahan** | ~**350 km** SE of Tehran; three **Chinese-built** research reactors | Tomahawk

[4] h: Reactions, retaliation and ceasefire

  [5.2] **UN Charter Article 2** | Bars the use of force
  [5.3] Iran's retaliation | Struck the US **Al-Udeid Air Base, Qatar** (heart of **CENTCOM**) a day after the US strikes – **with advance notice, no casualties**
  [5.4] Ceasefire | Trump announced a "**Complete and Total Ceasefire**" – war ended after **12 days**; **Israel's campaign lasted 11 days** (IRGC HQ, air defences, leadership)
  [5.5] Iran's missile | Solid-fuel **Kheybar Shekan**
  [5.6] Restraint | **No blockade of Hormuz**; proxies stayed silent
  [5.7] India's stakes | Hormuz ~**40%** of India's oil imports; **8 million+ Indians** in West Asia; **~50%** of crude from West Asia

  [8.1] India's exposure | US generic exports ~**$20 bn**; India supplies ~**40%** of US generics; US = ~**30%** of India's pharma exports; **$10.5 bn+** formulations to the US in FY25
  [8.2] **EU and Japan** | **Capped at 15%** – together ~**75%** of US pharma imports; affected: specialty drugs (Danish **Wegovy, Ozempic**); UK, Switzerland, Singapore may face the full 100%
  [8.3] Generics | **90% of US prescriptions but 13% of spending**; **APIs** inclusion uncertain – **PhRMA** objected

  [3.2] **First** India–Australia Defence Ministers' Dialogue | **Canberra, 9 Oct 2025**
  [3.3] **First** ASRAAM assembly and testing facility in India | **Hyderabad**
  [3.4] **Largest** US base in the Middle East | **Al-Udeid (Qatar)**
  [3.5] EU's **3rd** Security and Defence Partnership in Asia | India
  [3.6] EU as an FTA partner | India's **22nd**
### part 10: High-probability MCQ Traps
[0] table: Fact | Key detail
  [0.0] Iran's nuclear sites | **Natanz = main enrichment**; **Fordow = underground** near Qom (GBU-57 from B-2)
  [0.1] Kabul | Mission upgraded to an **embassy**; the Taliban regime is **not recognised** by India
  [0.2] Qatar | The **Qatar executive order is not a treaty** – no Senate approval; can be repealed
  [0.3] **Durand Line (1893)** | **Never recognised** by Afghanistan; Doha ceasefire mediated by **Qatar and Türkiye**
  [0.4] NPT | **Israel is not an NPT signatory**; Iran signed **1968**, ratified **1970** – no formal exit
  [0.5] NAM | **NAM (Belgrade 1961) ≠ neutrality**; strategic autonomy = multi-alignment
  [0.6] US pharma tariff | **Excludes generics**
  [0.7] Nepal's claim | **Kalapani, Limpiyadhura, Lipulekh**; **Sugauli Treaty 1815**
  [0.8] State–prefecture | **Andhra Pradesh – Toyama**
  [0.9] Quad | **No China**
  [0.10] **1974 Protocol** on shrine visits | **Non-binding**
  [0.11] Katchatheevu | Went to Sri Lanka by the **1974** treaty

### part 6: India's Foreign Policy Doctrines
[0] h: Doctrines – origin and essence
[1] table: Doctrine | Origin | Essence / feature
  [1.0] **Neighbourhood First** | **2014** (SAARC leaders invited to the PM's swearing-in) | Priority to neighbours: aid, infrastructure, connectivity, disaster response; used with MAHASAGAR
  [1.1] **Act East** | **2014**, East Asia Summit, **Nay Pyi Taw**; upgraded from **Look East (1991)** | Strategic ties with ASEAN, Japan, South Korea; meets Thailand's "**Act West**"; Act East Forum with Japan **2017**
  [1.2] **Link West** | – | India's own Gulf / West Asia policy: energy and diaspora ties
  [1.3] **IPOI** (Indo-Pacific Oceans Initiative) | East Asia Summit, **Bangkok, 2019** | **7 pillars**
  [1.4] **SAGAR** | **2015**, PM Modi | **Security and Growth for All in the Region** – maritime doctrine for the **Indian Ocean Region (IOR)**; India as net security provider
  [1.5] **MAHASAGAR** | **March 2025**, **Mauritius** | **Mutual and Holistic Advancement for Security and Growth Across Regions**; extends SAGAR to the **Global South** and the Indo-Pacific
  [1.6] **SHANTI** | **2026** | **Securing Holistic Advancement through Norms, Trust, and Integrity** – cooperative maritime security in the **Bay of Bengal**
  [1.7] **Nuclear doctrine** | **4 January 2003**, **Cabinet Committee on Security** (after **Pokhran-II**) | **Credible minimum deterrence**; **No First Use** (retaliation only), massive retaliation; no use against non-nuclear-weapon states; use authorised only by the civilian **Nuclear Command Authority**
[2] table: Fact | Key detail
  [2.0] Doctrine status | Unchanged despite **12 "operationally deployed" warheads** (30 Jun 2026)
  [2.1] **Nuclear Command Authority** | **Political Council** chaired by the **PM**; **Executive Council** chaired by the **NSA**
  [2.2] **Strategic Forces Command** | Manages the arsenal
[3] x: SAGAR (**2015**) = IOR; MAHASAGAR (**2025**) = Global South; SHANTI (**2026**) = Bay of Bengal.
[4] h: Related terms
[5] table: Term | Key detail
  [5.0] **Soft Power** | Coined by **Joseph Nye** (late 1980s): shaping others' choices through attraction and persuasion rather than force
  [5.1] **Minilateralism** | Term credited to **Miles Kahler (1992)**

### part 6: ISRO Missions, Launches and Engines
[0] h: EOS-05 (GISAT-1A) on GSLV-F17
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Launch | **4 September 2026**, **GSLV-F17**, **Second Launch Pad, SDSC-SHAR, Sriharikota**
  [1.1] Significance | **India's first imaging satellite from geosynchronous orbit**; images the subcontinent **every 30 minutes** (priority areas ~5 minutes) from ~**36,000 km**
  [1.2] Mass and orbit | ~**2,367 kg**; placed in **Sub-GTO** (perigee **170 km**, apogee **28,934 km**, inclination **19.28°**, azimuth **104°**), not directly in GEO
  [1.3] Uses | Agriculture, land resources, environmental and weather monitoring, disaster management
  [1.4] Edge over LEO / Sun-synchronous EO satellites | Watches the **same large area with very high temporal frequency** instead of once in **24** hours
[2] table: GSLV-F17 item | Key detail
  [2.0] Flight number | **19th** GSLV flight
  [2.1] Height / lift-off mass | **51.7 m** / **420.5 t**
  [2.2] Stages | **3**; **4 m** ogive fairing
  [2.3] GS1 | **S139** solid core + **4 L40H** liquid strap-ons
  [2.4] GS2 | **GL40HT** liquid stage
  [2.5] GS3 | **CUS15** cryogenic (LH2 + LOX)
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] **GSLV Mk II** capacity | ~**2,250 kg to GTO, 6,000 kg to LEO**
  [3.1] GSLV Mk II history | First flight **18 April 2001**; indigenous cryogenic stage from **GSLV-D5 (January 2014)**
  [3.2] **SDSC-SHAR** | Sriharikota, **Andhra Pradesh** – ISRO's principal launch centre; former name Sriharikota Range; named after **Prof. Satish Dhawan**
[4] h: Orbits compared
[5] table: Orbit | Key facts
  [5.0] LEO | **160–2,000 km**; one orbit **90–120 min**; ~**15,562** active payloads (4 Sep 2026); India has **22** operational LEO satellites (Apr 2026 ISRO report)
  [5.1] MEO | Galileo (Europe): **30** satellites
  [5.2] Geosynchronous | Period ~**23 h 56 min**; can be oval (elliptical) or tilted (inclined)
  [5.3] Geostationary | Circular, equatorial, zero inclination, ~**35,786 km**; period ~**24 h**; appears stationary – communications, weather, continuous regional monitoring; India has **31** GEO satellites
[6] b: Every geostationary orbit is geosynchronous, but a geosynchronous orbit need not be geostationary.
[7] h: Semi-cryogenic Power Head Test Article (PHTA)
[8] table: Fact | Key detail
  [8.0] Hot test | **100% rated level = 200 tonnes thrust** (47% level = **94 t**) at the **ISRO Propulsion Research Complex (IPRC), Mahendragiri, Tamil Nadu**
  [8.1] Place in series | **9th hot test**; **first at full rated level**; total **35 seconds**, **5 seconds** of it at full level
  [8.2] Validated | Switch from the low-pressure start tank to the medium-pressure run tank
  [8.3] PHTA | Includes **all engine systems except the thrust chamber**
  [8.4] **SE2000** | **2,000 kN**-class engine powering the **SC120** semi-cryogenic stage
  [8.5] SC120 | To replace the **L110 core stage of LVM3**; with the uprated **C32** cryogenic upper stage it raises LVM3's payload
[9] table: Type | Fuel | Engines / stages
  [9.0] Semi-cryogenic | Refined hydrocarbon + LOX | SE2000 / SC120
  [9.1] Cryogenic | LH2 + LOX | CUS15, C32
[10] h: Oceansat-3 (EOS-06) tracks El Niño
[11] table: Fact | Key detail
  [11.0] Finding | **EOS-06 / Oceansat-3** saw **weaker easterly winds (westerly reversals)** and **lower chlorophyll-a** in **June 2026** – a developing **El Niño** in the tropical Pacific
  [11.1] Analysis | **NRSC (ISRO)**, April–June data, **2023–2026**; combined **OCM-3 chlorophyll + scatterometer winds**; productive zone **contracted westward**
  [11.2] Launch | **26 November 2022**, **PSLV-C54**, Sriharikota; polar Sun-synchronous orbit; 3rd-generation Oceansat
  [11.3] Payloads | **Ocean Colour Monitor-3, Ku-band Scatterometer (SCAT-3), Sea Surface Temperature Monitor, ARGOS**
  [11.4] El Niño effect | Weakens upwelling → lower phytoplankton
[12] h: ISRO partnerships
[13] table: Fact | Key detail
  [13.0] **TRISHNA** | **Thermal InfraRed Imaging Satellite for High-resolution Natural resource Assessment**; **ISRO + CNES** (France) – thermal infrared (**TIR**) instrument by **CNES**, VNIR-SWIR by ISRO; launch next year on **PSLV**
  [13.1] **ISRO–ESA agreement** | In force since **January 2002**; **extended until 8 January 2032** by an exchange of letters at the International Space Summit, Paris, **10 Sep 2026**

### part 8: Nuclear Power – on Earth and in Space
[0] h: India's nuclear programme
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Target | AEC Chairman **Ajit Kumar Mohanty**: **100 GW by 2047**; private investment ~**₹7.5 lakh crore** for ~**30 GW**; ~₹25 crore per MW
  [1.1] **NPCIL** | 24 reactors (~9 GW); 17 under construction (13 GW); est. 1987, Mumbai
  [1.2] Thorium | India has ~**25% of world thorium**, in the **monazite sands** of Kerala, TN, Odisha, AP – Stage III of **Homi Bhabha's three stages**
  [1.3] **SMRs** | Up to 300 MW – for steel, cement
  [1.4] **Nuclear microreactors** | Very small reactors – resilient, **zero-carbon** power with **'walk-away' safety** that inherently prevents meltdowns; suit disaster areas and remote communities
  [1.5] **PRANIK** (3 Nov 2026) | **Pranos Fusion** (Bengaluru, 2024) plans **India's first private fusion reactor**; design by 2027; small tokamak **PRAGYA**; HTS magnets
  [1.6] Fusion background | ITER at Cadarache, France (India one of seven members); DAE 1954; Institute for Plasma Research, Gandhinagar
[2] h: Nuclear power in space
[3] table: System | How it works | Examples / use
  [3.0] **RTG** (Radioisotope Thermoelectric Generator) | Decay heat of plutonium-238 → electricity; no moving parts | Voyager 1 & 2, Curiosity, Perseverance
  [3.1] **RHU** (Radioisotope Heater Unit) | Heat only – no electricity | Keeps instruments from freezing
  [3.2] Fission reactor | Controlled chain reaction → much higher power | Lunar/Martian habitats
  [3.3] **NTP** (Nuclear Thermal Propulsion) | Reactor heats liquid hydrogen | ~Twice the efficiency of chemical rockets
  [3.4] **NEP** (Nuclear Electric Propulsion) | Electricity accelerates ionised propellant | Low thrust, very high efficiency
[4] table: Fact | Key detail
  [4.0] History | Radioisotope systems used since **1961**; Voyager launched in **1977** for a **5-year** mission
  [4.1] Plans | NASA (Aug 2025): lunar fission system ≥100 kW by Q1 FY2030; White House memo (Apr 2026): 20-kW reactors in orbit by 2028, on the Moon by 2030; China and Russia: Moon reactor by the mid-2030s for the **International Lunar Research Station**
  [4.2] Pu-238 shortage | US restarted production in 2015 after ~3 decades
[5] table: Instrument | Key point
  [5.0] Outer Space Treaty 1967 | Bans nuclear weapons/WMD in orbit; peaceful nuclear power is allowed
  [5.1] Liability Convention 1972 | Launching State absolutely liable for damage on Earth / to aircraft in flight
  [5.2] UN Principles on NPS 1992 | Safety, radiation protection, re-entry notification – only non-propulsive use
  [5.3] COPUOS–IAEA Safety Framework 2009 | Guidance – non-binding
[6] b: **Cosmos 954:** Soviet satellite re-entered over northern Canada (1978); settled 1981 for C$3 million.

### part 0: Digital India, Quantum Mission and Semiconductors
[0] h: National Quantum Mission (NQM)
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Sanction | **April 2023**; **₹6,003.65 crore** (2023–31); nodal **DST**
  [1.1] Scope | **Computing, communication, sensing and materials**; **four** thematic hubs (T-Hubs); **17 start-ups**

[2] table: T-Hub | Domain
  [2.0] IISc Bengaluru | Computing
  [2.1] IIT Madras + C-DOT | Communication
  [2.2] **IIT Bombay** | **Sensing**

[4] h: Quantum basics
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] **Qubit** | 0, 1 or both (**superposition**); also **entanglement** and **interference**
  [5.1] **Quantum Error Correction** | Many **physical qubits** combined to form **one logical qubit**
  [5.2] **QKD** | Uses **photons**, the **observer effect** and the **no-cloning rule**
  [5.3] **IBM** | Aims at a fault-tolerant quantum computer by **2029**
  [5.4] **Physics Nobel 2025** | **John Clarke, Michel H. Devoret, John M. Martinis** (macroscopic quantum tunnelling)
[6] table: Qubit type | Basis
  [6.0] Superconducting | Josephson junctions (Google Willow)
  [6.1] **Trapped-ion** | **Charged atoms held in electromagnetic fields**
  [6.2] Quantum-dot | Semiconductors
  [6.3] Photonic | Photons
[7] h: Semiconductors and electronics
[8] table: Fact | Key detail
  [8.0] **ATMP** / **OSAT** | Assembly, Testing, Marking, Packaging / Outsourced Semiconductor Assembly and Test
  [8.1] **Micron ATMP, Sanand, Gujarat** | Commercial production; a step towards digital sovereignty
  [8.2] **Vihaan** | India's **first domestically designed broadband-networking System-on-Chip**; Chennai-based **Ahisa Digital Innovations**, under MeitY's **DLI scheme**; first-pass silicon success with open **RISC-V** and C-DAC's VEGA processor
  [8.3] **NIDAR 2.0** | **MeitY + Drone Federation of India** (July 2026); drones with **VEGA**, the indigenous **RISC-V** processor
  [8.4] **MPMP-S** (Mobile Phone Manufacturing Scheme) | ₹62,500 crore, FY 2026–27 to 2030–31; **extra 1.5%** incentive for Indian-made key components
  [8.5] **NIPU-2026** (urea investment policy) | Gas-based plants; **fixed RoE 12–16%**
[9] h: Digital India (launched 2015; 11 years on 1 July 2026)
[10] table: Fact | Key detail
  [10.0] **DPI** | UPI, RuPay, Aadhaar, DigiLocker, ONDC
  [10.1] Liabilities | **Digital Rupee = RBI liability**; **UPI money = bank deposit liability**
  [10.2] **BharatNet** | **~97%** of targeted GPs (2.15 of 2.22 lakh)
  [10.3] Numbers | **Aadhaar 144 crore+**; **DBT ₹51 lakh crore+**; PDS authentication **98%+**; Jan Dhan **57.78 crore** accounts, deposits **₹2.94 lakh crore**
  [10.4] **MeghRaj** (2014) | Government cloud; **342 → 2,323 departments**
  [10.5] **DIKSHA** | Built on **Sunbird**
  [10.6] **India Stack Global** and **Global DPI Repository** | Launched during India's **G20 Presidency (2023)**
  [10.7] **Global Innovation Index 2025** | India **38th** (81st in 2015); R&D spend ~**0.74% of GDP** vs world ~2.07%
  [10.8] Digital-sovereignty trigger | **EseeCloud** (**China**) – Indian CCTV networks compromised (April 2026)
### part 1: ISRO Missions, Gaganyaan and the Space Roadmap
[0] h: Missions so far
[1] table: Mission | Key fact
  [1.0] Chandrayaan-1 (2008) | Evidence of **water/hydroxyl** on the Moon
  [1.1] Chandrayaan-3 (23 Aug 2023) | **First near the lunar south pole**; **4th** country to soft-land (after US, Russia, China)
  [1.2] Mangalyaan | Launched **5 Nov 2013**; Mars orbit **24 September 2014** – **first to reach Mars orbit in its maiden attempt**
  [1.3] Aditya-L1 (2023) | First solar observatory; **halo orbit around L1** (~**1.5 million km**)
  [1.4] **AstroSat** | **First multi-wavelength space observatory**
  [1.5] XPoSat (1 Jan 2024) | **X-ray astronomy**
  [1.6] South Asia Satellite (GSAT-9) | **5 May 2017** – Afghanistan, Bangladesh, Nepal, Sri Lanka, **Bhutan**, Maldives
  [1.7] Aryabhata | India's first satellite, **19 April 1975**, with Soviet help
  [1.8] **RLV-TD** | **Three** autonomous runway landings
[2] h: Future missions and Space Vision 2047

[0] h: Drugs and vaccines

[2] h: Nipah and other infections
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] June 2026 case | 43-year-old in **Kozhikode**
  [3.1] Reservoir | **Indian flying fox bat (Pteropus medius)**
  [3.2] Kerala outbreaks | First **2018** – 23 cases, CFR 91%; later 2019, 2021, 2023 (Kozhikode cluster), 2024–25
  [3.3] **One Health Centre for Nipah** | **Kozhikode, 2023**; 2.5 lakh+ volunteers; Nipah is a WHO priority pathogen
  [3.4] **Whooping cough** | Bacterium ***Bordetella pertussis***
  [3.5] **Rotavirus** | Genus in the family **Reoviridae**; leading cause of severe diarrhoea in **children under 5**; spreads by the **faecal-oral route**
[4] h: Generative biology and agri-biotech
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] **Stanford + Arc Institute** | Genome models Evo 1 and Evo 2 designed ΦX174-like bacteriophage genomes – **285 tested in E. coli, 16 functional**; AI **proposed** the designs, humans built and tested them
  [5.1] Genome stages | **Reading 1970s** → writing 2000s → editing 2010s → AI design now
  [5.2] Uses | **Phage therapy against AMR**; **functional DNA screening** checks **what a sequence does**
  [5.3] **Synthetic Biology (SynBio)** | Combines **engineering with molecular biology** to design or build new biological parts and systems; builds **multi-gene networks** (genetic engineering: a single gene); driven by CRISPR, AI, cheap DNA synthesis
  [5.4] **ICRISAT** | **ICGV 181030** = India's first **high-oleic groundnut** (7–12% higher yield)
  [5.5] **Blue Swimmer Crab** farming | India's first technology, **ICAR-CIBA, Chennai**
  [5.6] Low-cost **Zinc-Air battery** | **Dr S. Devaraj, SASTRA**
[6] h: Banned and controlled substances
[7] table: Fact | Key detail
  [7.0] **2-Bromo-4-Methylpropiophenone** | Notified as a **controlled substance under the NDPS Act** (Ministry of Finance) – precursor for **mephedrone ('Meow Meow', M-CAT)**
  [7.1] **Chloramphenicol and Nitrofurans** | Banned (**March 2025**) in **aquaculture and food-producing animals**; action stepped up in **shrimp farming** after export rejections; chloramphenicol residues can cause **aplastic anaemia**

### part 11: Energy and Technology Firsts – Hydrogen Train, Geothermal, Helium and EVs
[0] h: India's first hydrogen train
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Launch | Flagged off at **Jind, Haryana**, **17 July 2026**; route **Jind–Sonipat, 89 km**; under **"Hydrogen for Heritage"**
  [1.1] Technology | **PEM (Proton Exchange Membrane) fuel cell**; power **2,400 kW** (**1,200 kW** per power car)
  [1.2] **Jind** | India's largest railway hydrogen **storage and refuelling** centre
  [1.3] World's first hydrogen passenger train | **Alstom Coradia iLint (Germany, 2018)**
  [1.4] **National Green Hydrogen Mission** | Jan **2023**, **₹19,744 crore**, **MNRE**
[2] h: Geothermal – Puga Valley
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] **India's first and deepest geothermal wells** | **Puga Valley, Ladakh** (July 2026); **1 MW** demonstration; by the **ONGC** Energy Centre; Carbon-Neutral Ladakh
  [3.1] **National Policy on Geothermal Energy, 2025** | MNRE – supports **Net Zero 2070**; potential ~**10.6 GW**
  [3.2] Sites | Puga (Ladakh), Manikaran (HP), Tapoban (Uttarakhand), **Yumesamdong (Sikkim)**
  [3.3] Plant types | **Dry steam, flash, binary**
[4] h: Helium
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] Supply | **US ~43%** of world helium; China only ~**1.6%** (China's export ban, July 2026)
  [5.1] Properties | Atomic number **2**; **Group 18** noble gas; boiling point ~**–269°C**; **cannot solidify at normal pressure** (needs ~**25 atm** at ~**1 K**)
  [5.2] Uses | **Quantum computers**, MRI magnets, semiconductor wafers, rocket tank pressurisation; in **scuba tanks** to avoid **nitrogen narcosis**
[6] h: Electric vehicles and batteries

  [7.0] EV sales | ~**23 lakh (2025)**; EV adoption **0.08% (FY16) → 8.26% (FY26)**

  [8.1] HEV | Engine + motor
  [8.2] **PHEV** | **Hybrid with plug-in battery**

## topic group2 6-21 | Mar–May 2026: Science and technology in news - space missions, defence tests, health and biotech developments
### part 0: Indian Space Programme - Gaganyaan, ISRO and NavIC
[0] h: Gaganyaan – India's first human spaceflight
[1] table: Particular | Key detail
  [1.0] Crew and orbit | **3** astronauts to LEO at about **400 km** for up to **3 days**
  [1.1] Launcher | Human-rated **LVM3** (GSLV Mk III) – solid, liquid and cryogenic stages
  [1.2] LVM3 stages | **2** solid strap-ons, liquid core stage, cryogenic upper stage
  [1.3] Orbital Module | Crew Module (pressurised, habitable, thermal protection) + Service Module (unpressurised; separates before re-entry)
  [1.4] Crew Escape System | Validated by **TV-D1**; uses solid rocket motors; works during launch and ascent
  [1.5] Vyommitra | Female (semi-)humanoid robot for the uncrewed flights **G1/G2**
  [1.6] Re-entry | Speed falls from about **7.8 km/s**, mainly by atmospheric drag (aerobraking)
  [1.7] Splashdown | **Bay of Bengal**; the sea absorbs a **7-9 m/s** impact (against **1-2 m/s** needed on land)
  [1.8] Tests | IADT (Integrated Air Drop Test), Pad Abort Test, Test Vehicle flights
  [1.9] Significance | India would join the USA, Russia and China with independent human spaceflight capability
[2] table: Fact | Key detail
  [2.0] **IADT-02** | Second Gaganyaan Integrated Air Drop Test at SDSC on **10 April 2026**, ahead of uncrewed G1; an IADT is **not an orbital flight** (uncrewed orbital flights are G1 onwards)
  [2.1] **IADT-1** (**24 August 2025**) | Validated the **parachute-based deceleration system** of the crew module – a **5-tonne** dummy module dropped from **3.1 km** by an **IAF Chinook**; main parachutes deployed in sequence
  [2.2] **Mission Mitra** | Gaganyaan **analog mission in Ladakh** (isolation, low oxygen); modelled on ESA's **CAVES**
  [2.3] **AIIMS-ISRO MoU (March 2026)** | **Space medicine** for Gaganyaan
  [2.4] G1 status (Aug 2026) | Uncrewed G1 "launching shortly"; crew-module tests done; **ESA** to give ground-tracking support
  [2.5] Institutions | Human Space Flight Centre (HSFC): est. **2019, Bengaluru**; IN-SPACe: est. **2020, Ahmedabad**; ISRO Chairman **V. Narayanan**
[3] x: Gaganyaan flies on the **human-rated LVM3**; the **Crew Escape System** works at **launch and ascent**.
[4] h: Other ISRO missions and agreements
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] **Aditya-L1** | India's **first solar observatory**, at **L1 (about 1.5 million km)**; journey about **125 days** (against about **300** days for MOM); ISRO's **second observatory-class mission after AstroSat (2015)**
  [5.1] **ISRO-ESA agreement (March 2026)** | **Earth-observation calibration**; ties since **1978**; ESA's **FLEX** mission; ESA est. 1975, Paris
  [5.2] **Akasha300** | 3D printer by a **Kerala startup** delivered to ISRO (supported by STIIC at IIST and KSUM)
  [5.3] Microgravity research | **Axiom-4** and **Gaganyaan** support it; **SpaDeX** is a **docking technology demonstration** (its POEM platform carried experiments)
[6] h: NavIC – IRNSS-1F loses its atomic clock
[7] table: Fact | Key detail
  [7.0] Failure | The atomic clock on **IRNSS-1F stopped on 13 March 2026** – no positioning data; it **continues broadcast messaging**
  [7.1] IRNSS-1F | Launched **10 March 2016**; completed its **10-year design life**; all **three** atomic clocks now non-functional
  [7.2] Constellation | After NVS-01 (2023), **five** satellites gave positioning: IRNSS-1B, 1C, 1F, 1I and NVS-01; NavIC needs **at least four** operational satellites; services began **2018**
[8] table: NavIC fact | Key detail
  [8.0] Official name | IRNSS; planned **7**-satellite constellation
  [8.1] Coverage / accuracy | India + **1,500 km**; about **10 m**
  [8.2] Orbit | Geosynchronous (~**35,000 km**); global systems use MEO (~**20,000 km**)
  [8.3] NVS (new generation) | Indigenous atomic clock; **12-year** design life; L1, L5, S bands (L1 enables smartphones/wearables)
  [8.4] NVS-02 | Launched January **2025** by **GSLV-F15** – ISRO's **100th** launch; failed to reach its final orbit (signal did not reach the pyro valve of the oxidiser line – connector disengagement)
  [8.5] Earlier failures | IRNSS-1H (**2017**) – heat shield did not open; IRNSS-1A (**2013**) almost defunct
[9] h: Navigation systems by country
[10] table: System | Country
  [10.0] GPS | USA
  [10.1] GLONASS | Russia
  [10.2] Galileo | EU
  [10.3] BeiDou | China
  [10.4] NavIC (regional) | India
  [10.5] QZSS (regional) | Japan
[11] b: **GAGAN** – India's **SBAS**, by **ISRO + Airports Authority of India**; used beyond aviation.
[12] x: Regional systems (NavIC, QZSS) = geosynchronous orbit; global systems (GPS, GLONASS, Galileo, BeiDou) = MEO.
[13] h: Private space sector and space infrastructure
[14] table: Item | Key detail
  [14.0] GalaxEye (Bengaluru) | India's largest private satellite and the world's first **OptoSAR** satellite (optical + SAR); launched on SpaceX Falcon-9 from California; India = **4th** nation with this technology
  [14.1] Pathfinder | Pixxel + Sarvam (announced **4 May 2026**) – India's first AI-powered orbital data centre satellite; single-satellite demonstrator; Pixxel builds and operates it (hyperspectral payload), Sarvam gives the AI backbone; scheduled for Q4 **2026**
  [14.2] Vikram-1 | Skyroot Aerospace – flight hardware unveiled by Telangana CM Revanth Reddy (April 2026), moved from Max-Q Campus, Hyderabad, to SDSC Sriharikota; up to **350 kg** to LEO; flew on **18 July 2026** (Mission Aagaman)
  [14.3] Project Garuda | Dhruva Space (Hyderabad) – **₹105 crore** grant from the Research Development and Innovation Fund for a standard **500-kg**-class satellite platform
  [14.4] Karnataka Space Tech CoE | India's first State-led Space Technology Centre of Excellence, Bengaluru – with SIA-India and the Karnataka Science and Technology Society; theme "Space Tech for Societal Impact"
  [14.5] Two Common Technical Facilities | DoS approved the Space Manufacturing Park at **Khoraj** (near Ahmedabad, Gujarat) and the Space Vehicles Cluster at **Thoothukudi** (near the Kulasekarapattinam spaceport, TN); IN-SPACe to operationalise
  [14.6] Space Venture Capital Fund | **₹1,005 crore** (Minister Jitendra Singh); SEBI AIF; investor IN-SPACe, sponsor SIDBI; investments from FY**2027**
  [14.7] NSIL-Dhruva Space MoU | Space-grade solar panels; NSIL est. **6 March 2019**
  [14.8] BSE-2 | Abhyom Spacetech reusable liquid engine – **2.5 kN**, **6 s**; tested at BITS Pilani Hyderabad; for the HOPE vehicle
  [14.9] Project NETRA | Optical telescope at **Hanle** (Ladakh) for debris tracking; Indian Space Situational Awareness (ISSA) since **2019**; Hanle – India's first dark sky reserve
  [14.10] X-band Doppler radar | **Mahabaleshwar** – under Mission Mausam (IITM Pune)
[15] b: Skyroot co-founders: **Pawan Kumar Chandana, Naga Bharath Daka**; Pixxel: est. **2019**, Bengaluru.
### part 1: Missiles and Weapon Tests
[0] h: Strategic and guided weapons
[1] table: System | Key detail
  [1.0] **Agni with MIRV** | Tested from APJ Abdul Kalam Island, Odisha; hit multiple targets in the Indian Ocean region; possibly under Mission Divyastra with Agni-5 (~5,000 km); re-entry speed 20–24 times the speed of sound; decoy warheads; India joins the US, Russia, China, France, UK
  [1.1] **TARA** (Tactical Advanced Range Augmentation) | India's first indigenous Guided Glide Weapon – converts unguided warheads into precision-guided munitions; DRDO + IAF trial off Odisha; developed by RCI, Hyderabad
  [1.2] **PGM-V3** | UAV-launched precision guided missile; air-to-surface and air-to-air variants validated at DRDO's **Kurnool** test facility (AP); targets tanks, drones, helicopters
  [1.3] **NASM-SR** (Naval Anti-Ship Missile-Short Range) | First salvo launch – two missiles in sequence from one naval helicopter, Bay of Bengal off Odisha (DRDO + Navy)
  [1.4] Scramjet combustor | DRDO long-duration test of 1,200+ seconds (beat 700 s of January 2026); fully scaled, actively cooled; by DRDL, Hyderabad; for hypersonic cruise missiles (Mach 5+)
  [1.5] HSTDV | DRDO's hypersonic technology demonstrator programme
  [1.6] **Dhvani** | **Hypersonic Glide Vehicle** under development by **DRDO**
  [1.7] Vayu Baan | IAF's first helicopter-launched drone system (Air-Launched Effects); strikes beyond 50 km
  [1.8] First indigenous heavy torpedo | Delivered by BDL (Hyderabad, est. 1970) to the Navy at Visakhapatnam
  [1.9] ADC-150 | Air-Droppable Container (150 kg) tested off Goa from a P-8I; nodal lab NSTL, Visakhapatnam

[10] h: Missile interceptors in the US–Iran war
[11] table: Fact | Key detail
  [11.0] Expanded IAMD network | US + Israel + UAE against Iran – unlike the **Twelve-Day War (June 2025)**, when Iran fired 500+ ballistic missiles and twice as many suicide drones
  [11.1] **UAE** | Deployed **South Korean Cheongung II** over the Gulf
  [11.2] **Cheongung II** | **Hit-to-kill**; **360° firing via Vertical Launch System** + **rotating multi-function radar**; nose radar in the final seconds against low-flying "skimmers"; **older Patriot radars scanned only a 120° cone** (stationary radar)
  [11.3] **US IFPC** (Indirect Fire Protection Capability) | Uses **AIM-9X Sidewinder** to depend less on Patriot; **SM-6** has a dual role (terminal interceptor + anti-ship/fast-attack craft)
  [11.4] **Israel's Iron Beam** (laser) | Primary defence against **drone swarms**, to save **Arrow 3 and Stunner** interceptors
  [11.5] **THAAD** | Terminal High Altitude Area Defence – an **American** anti-missile system
  [11.6] Patriot PAC-3 MSE | ~$4 million per interceptor (cost asymmetry vs cheap drones); once hit 100% of six hypersonic Kinzhals over Kyiv but fell to ~10% against Russian decoys
  [11.7] Orders | US quadrupled orders for THAAD and PAC-3 MSE (THAAD replenishment at least 1.5 years)

  [12.2] Mid layer | David's Sling with Stunner
  [12.3] Last line | Patriot
  [12.4] Against drones | Iron Dome (Tamir) + Iron Beam

[10] table: System | Key detail

  [10.5] **Cell Broadcast System (CBS)** | Indigenous; launched by the Ministry of Communications, developed by **C-DOT** (est. **1984**, New Delhi); sends alerts **to all phones in an area at once**; **unaffected by network congestion** (unlike SMS)
  [10.6] **SACHET** | DoT + NDMA **multilingual SMS alert system** by C-DOT; uses the **Common Alerting Protocol (CAP) of the ITU** (est. **1865**, Geneva)
  [10.7] NDMA | Est. **2005**, chaired by the PM
[11] x: **BFS** = **6 km** resolution (old model **12 km**). **CBS** reaches all phones at once; SMS alerts can be hit by congestion.

[7] h: Gut microbiome
[8] table: Fact | Key detail
  [8.0] Size | Trillions of microbes (~equal to human cells), 1–2 kg; **2–20 million microbial genes vs ~20,000 human genes**
  [8.1] Species | ~2,000 bacterial species, **mostly in the colon**; ~90% beneficial (Bacteroidetes, Firmicutes)
  [8.2] Roles | SCFAs reduce inflammation; makes vitamin K and some B vitamins; gut–brain axis; dysbiosis → "leaky gut"
  [8.3] **Extra sugar** | Helps **E. coli and Candida**; reduces **Bifidobacteria and Lactobacilli**
  [8.4] **Estrobolome** | Gut bacteria regulating estrogen metabolism – disrupted by **pesticides**
  [8.5] AMR | Antibiotics in livestock → AMR; bacteria, viruses and fungi can all develop AMR
  [8.6] Enzymes | Fat – lipases; proteins – trypsin/chymotrypsin; starch – amylase

### part 6: World Space, Astronomy and Space Governance
[0] h: Artemis programme
[1] table: Mission | Key detail
  [1.0] Artemis I (2022) | Uncrewed Orion around the Moon
  [1.1] Artemis II | Launched **1 April 2026**, Kennedy Space Center – first crewed Artemis flight; lunar flyby in Orion (crew named it "Integrity"), four astronauts, **no landing**; first crewed lunar flyby in **50+** years
  [1.2] Artemis III | Landing (target **2028**; about **2027-28**)
  [1.3] Artemis IV, V | **2028-29**, **2030**; NASA to use Starship for Artemis-4 (Moon, 2028)
[2] table: Fact | Key detail
  [2.0] **Artemis II crew** | **Reid Wiseman, Victor Glover, Christina Koch** and **Jeremy Hansen (Canadian Space Agency)**
  [2.1] Artemis II record | **Farthest humans from Earth – 252,760 miles**, beating **Apollo 13 (248,655 miles)**; will observe the far side; prepares for lunar South Pole missions; delays came from hydrogen leaks
  [2.2] NASA lunar plans | A Moon landing every six months and a permanent lunar base: about **US$20 bn** multi-phase plan, astronauts by **2028**
  [2.3] Lunar missions | **Blue Origin Blue Moon Mark 1 Endurance** lander; **Astrobotic Griffin** (~**1,100 lb**, Astrolab FLIP rover); **Lunar Vertex** (lunar swirls)
  [2.4] Apollo context | First landing **1969**; **12 astronauts** walked on the Moon in **six** missions (1969-72); ISRO was founded within a month of Apollo 11
  [2.5] ISS and Moon | ISS about **400 km** up, crewed for ~**25** years, to retire in **3-4** years; Moon ~**400,000 km** away
  [2.6] **Space Reactor-1 Freedom** | Nuclear-powered spacecraft to Mars in **2028**
  [2.7] Human landings planned | China by **2030** (Chang'e); **India by 2040** (Space Vision 2047)
  [2.8] **Starship-V3** | SpaceX test from Starbase, Texas, with **20** dummy satellites; fell into the Indian Ocean
[3] h: SMILE – ESA and China
[4] table: Fact | Key detail
  [4.0] **SMILE** | Solar wind Magnetosphere Ionosphere Link Explorer – joint mission of **ESA** and the **Chinese Academy of Sciences (CAS)**; their **first full-scale joint space mission**
  [4.1] Launch | **19 May 2026** on **Vega-C** from Europe's Spaceport, **French Guiana**; mission life about **three years**
  [4.2] Aim | **First mission to observe Earth's magnetosphere in X-ray light**; studies Earth's response to solar storms and CMEs
  [4.3] Agencies | ESA: est. **1975**, HQ Paris; CAS: est. **1949**, HQ Beijing
  [4.4] Magnetosphere | Region controlled by Earth's magnetic field; deflects the solar wind; **CMEs and solar flares cause space weather**; **auroras are seen near the poles**
[5] h: Astronomy in news
[6] table: Item | Key detail
  [6.0] Zwaan-Wolf effect | First detected in a planetary atmosphere – Mars, by NASA MAVEN (launched **2013**); effect identified **1976**
  [6.1] FRB 20240304B | Fast Radio Burst that travelled ~**10 bn** years; detected **4 March 2024** by MeerKAT (South Africa); host dwarf galaxy found with JWST; from Cosmic Noon (Universe ~**3 bn** years old)
  [6.2] Hydroxyl megamaser | Detected by MeerKAT about **8 billion** light-years away (March 2026)
  [6.3] Curiosity rover | Carbon compounds on Mars – Gale Crater; launched **26 Nov 2011**
  [6.4] Cygnus X-1 | Jet speed measured for the first time – **540 million km/h** (~half light speed); ~**7,200** light-years; discovered **1964**
  [6.5] Nancy Grace Roman Telescope | Final assembly done; launch **2026**; **2.4 m** mirror; **100x** Hubble's field of view
  [6.6] PoET | Paranal Solar ESPRESSO Telescope – first light at ESO Paranal Observatory, Atacama Desert, Chile; works with the ESPRESSO spectrograph (used with the VLT at night); ESO est. **1962**, HQ Garching, Germany
[7] h: Space security and space law
[8] table: Term | Meaning
  [8.0] **Dual-use satellites** | Civilian satellites also usable for military purposes (e.g. GPS)
  [8.1] **Orbital dependency** | Reliance on foreign satellite infrastructure
  [8.2] **Jamming** / **Spoofing** | **Blocking** signals / sending **false** signals (e.g. fake GPS positions)
  [8.3] **KA-SAT cyberattack (2022)** | On the **Viasat** network; disrupted internet across **Europe**
  [8.4] **CERT-In space cybersecurity guidelines 2026** | **"Secure-by-design"** across the satellite lifecycle
  [8.5] **Kessler Syndrome** | Chain reaction of debris collisions making orbits unusable
  [8.6] Debris tracking | In LEO only objects **larger than 10 cm** are regularly tracked; **25-year de-orbit rule** (weak compliance monitoring)
[9] table: Instrument | Key point
  [9.0] Outer Space Treaty, **1967** | Foundation of space law; peaceful use, no WMD in orbit; states responsible for all national space activities, including private entities; space = global commons; silent on cyber operations
  [9.1] Liability Convention, **1972** | Compensation for damage by space objects – absolute liability on Earth/aircraft; fault-based in outer space
  [9.2] IADC, UN COPUOS guidelines | Debris mitigation – non-binding, voluntary

[0] h: Semiconductor Mission

[2] table: Fact | Key detail
  [2.0] **India's first semiconductor fab** | **Dholera SEZ, Gujarat** – **Tata Electronics with PSMC** (Taiwan); notified by **Tata Semiconductor Manufacturing Pvt Ltd**; Dholera SIR is under the Delhi–Mumbai Industrial Corridor; semiconductor zone ~166 ha
