  [2.2] **Odisha–Intel glass substrate** | MoUs with Intel and 3D Glass Solutions – Infovalley, Khordha; ₹1,943.53 crore (Centre ₹799 cr, State ₹399.5 cr); approved under ISM Aug 2025; production Aug 2028
  [2.3] **GAETEC, Hyderabad** (DRDO) | Makes **indigenous GaN (Gallium Nitride) chips**; on **13 May 2026 SAMEER and ISRO's ISTRAC** agreed to build **X-band deep-space communication amplifiers** with it; DRDO's **SSPL** developed 4-inch **SiC** wafer processes
  [2.4] **Terrafab** (Elon Musk) | Semiconductor project in **Austin, Texas** by **Tesla, SpaceX, xAI**; next-gen AI chips incl. Tesla AI5; **larger than Tesla Gigafactories**; to depend less on **Samsung, TSMC and Micron**
  [2.5] **Germanium** | Used in the first transistor (Bell Labs, 1947); **discovered 1886 by Clemens Winkler**; predicted by **Mendeleev as "eka-silicon"**
  [2.6] **Doping** | **Adding** controlled impurities to a semiconductor; semiconductor conductivity **rises** with temperature; silicon is a metalloid
[3] h: Quantum and AI terms
[4] table: Term | Meaning
  [4.0] **Quantum entanglement** | Shared quantum state – **measuring one particle's property (momentum, spin) instantly fixes its partner's, at any distance**; earlier shown mainly in **photons and electrons**, recently in heavier atoms like **Helium**
  [4.1] **Nonlocality** | A quantum feature; classical physics is based on locality
  [4.2] **Anthropic's Mythos** | AI model that can autonomously find and exploit software vulnerabilities; not public; shared selectively with critical-infrastructure organisations under **Project Glasswing**
  [4.3] **Zero-day vulnerability** | Flaw **unknown to developers, no patch** yet
  [4.4] **LLMs** | Use the **transformer** architecture; **temperature** controls randomness; **tokenization** splits text into words/subwords/characters (it does not decide the architecture); loss function measures prediction error
  [4.5] Other terms | Adversarial attacks, data poisoning, **XAI = Explainable AI**, EDR systems, deepfakes
  [4.6] **Neuro-Symbolic AI** | Hybrid of neural networks (patterns) + symbolic reasoning (rules, knowledge graphs) → explainable answers, fewer hallucinations
[5] b: **Neuro-symbolic examples:** **PrahelikaAI – IIT Kharagpur** (puzzle-based reasoning tutor in Hindi, Bengali); CREST framework, PAL (Personal Adaptive Learner), C3AN (offline edge AI). Only **47%** of rural schools have functional computers; ~1.4 million schools; 22 scheduled languages.
[6] h: Anthropic study – "Observed Exposure"
[7] table: Fact | Key detail
  [7.0] Measure | **Actual workplace use** of AI (not theoretical capability); ~800 US occupations and Claude usage
  [7.1] **Most exposed** | Computer programmers, financial analysts, customer service, data analysts, **legal**, office administration
  [7.2] **Least exposed** | **Construction, agriculture, protective services, personal care**
  [7.3] Gap | LLMs could theoretically do ~94% of computer/maths tasks; actual use ~33%
  [7.4] Jobs | Entry of 22–25-year-olds into exposed jobs down ~14% since 2022
  [7.5] India | **AI could cut IT-services revenue by 9–12% over four years** (Nifty IT fell)
### part 8: Nuclear Energy - PFBR, SHANTI Act and Tarapur
[0] h: Kalpakkam PFBR attains criticality
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Milestone | **Prototype Fast Breeder Reactor (PFBR), Kalpakkam** (Tamil Nadu) attained **first criticality on 6 April 2026** – **India enters Stage II** of its three-stage nuclear programme; PM Modi called it historic
  [1.1] Reactor | **500 MWe**, **sodium-cooled**; built by **BHAVINI** (Bharatiya Nabhikiya Vidyut Nigam Ltd, under **DAE**); **200+** Indian industries took part
  [1.2] **Criticality** | Self-sustaining chain reaction, **k = 1** (subcritical k < 1, supercritical k > 1); chain-reaction idea – **Leo Szilard (1933)**
  [1.3] **Fast breeder reactor** | Produces **more fissile fuel than it consumes**; **fast neutrons, no moderator**; **plutonium-based MOX fuel** (Pu + U); U-238 blanket → Pu-239; **liquid sodium** coolant (low dissolved oxygen for corrosion resistance)
  [1.4] Plants | Tarapur, Rajasthan, Kalpakkam, Narora, Kakrapar, Kaiga, Kudankulam; **Kalpakkam has MAPS and IGCAR**
[2] table: Stage (Homi J. Bhabha) | Reactor | Fuel
  [2.0] I | PHWRs | Natural uranium → Pu-239 by-product
  [2.1] II | FBRs | Pu-based MOX
  [2.2] III | Thorium reactors | Th-232 → U-233
[3] h: SHANTI Act, 2025
[4] table: Fact | Key detail
  [4.0] Full form | **Sustainable Harnessing and Advancement of Nuclear Energy for Transforming India**
  [4.1] Replaces | The **Atomic Energy Act, 1962** and the **Civil Liability for Nuclear Damage (CLND) Act, 2010**
  [4.2] Private role | **Private companies, JVs and foreign entities** can **build, own, operate and decommission** plants, including **SMRs and micro reactors** – ends the **NPCIL monopoly**
  [4.3] Liability | **Operator solely liable**; **supplier liability removed**; operator liability graded by capacity; government liability capped
  [4.4] Regulator | Gives **statutory (legal) status to the Atomic Energy Regulatory Board (AERB)**
  [4.5] Target | Nuclear capacity from about **8,180 MW to 100 GW by 2047** (over twelvefold); investment over **$200 billion**; nuclear share in power about **3%**
  [4.6] Installed electricity capacity (June 2025) | ~**476 GW**, ~**50%** non-fossil
[5] h: Tarapur
[6] table: Fact | Key detail
  [6.0] **Tarapur Units 1 and 2 (TAPS)** | Near Boisar, Palghar – **boiling water reactors (BWRs)**; commissioned **1969**; **India's first commercial reactors**
  [6.1] Restart | **AERB approved the restart of Unit-2 on 7 May 2026** for **10** more years (after NPCIL refurbishment); Unit-1 extended Dec 2025 (**160 MWe**)
  [6.2] Bodies | AERB: est. **1983**, Mumbai, Chairman A.K. Balasubramanian; NPCIL: est. **1987**, CMD Bhuvan Chandra Pathak; DAE: est. **1954**
[7] x: Tarapur 1 and 2 are **BWRs**; Stage I of the programme uses **PHWRs**.
### part 9: Naval, Coast Guard and Shipbuilding
[0] h: Ships and submarines
[1] table: Platform | Key detail
  [1.0] **INS Shachi** | Commissioned 31 March 2026 at **Goa** Shipyard – first of 11 NGOPVs
  [1.1] **INS Taragiri** | Commissioned 3 April 2026, **Visakhapatnam**; Project 17A stealth frigate; 6,670 t; built by MDL
  [1.2] **INS Aridaman** | Second indigenous nuclear-powered ballistic missile submarine (**SSBN**) (Arihant class, ATV project); commissioned at Visakhapatnam by Rajnath Singh; INS Arihant commissioned 2016; K-series missiles
  [1.3] **Dunagiri** | Project 17A Nilgiri-class **frigate** (GRSE) – BrahMos
  [1.4] **Sanshodhak** | **Survey ship** (GRSE)
  [1.5] **Agre** | Arnala-class **ASW** shallow water craft (GRSE)
  [1.6] INS Malvan | Second ASW-SWC by Cochin Shipyard (8-ship project)
  [1.7] Mahendragiri | Project 17A (Nilgiri-class) stealth guided-missile frigate built by MDL, delivered 30 April 2026 – the **sixth** P-17A frigate (the **fourth built at MDL**); named after Mahendragiri Hills, Odisha; commissioned at Visakhapatnam on 11 July 2026 (Eastern Fleet)
  [1.8] ICGS Achal | Inducted 9 May 2026 at Goa Shipyard Ltd – next-gen Adamya-class Fast Patrol Vessel, 50%+ indigenous; based at Vadinar, Gujarat (Coast Guard Region North West)
  [1.9] INS Sanghamitra | Launched 20 May 2026 by GRSE, Kolkata – first NGOPV launched by GRSE (Shachi-class), Buy (Indian-IDDM); ~113 m, 3,000 t, 23 knots, 8,500 nm; named after Ashoka's daughter; emblem Ursa Major and lighthouse
[2] table: Fact | Key detail
  [2.0] **Project 17A** | Follow-on to **Project 17 (Shivalik class)**; **seven** frigates – **4 by MDL (Mumbai), 3 by GRSE (Kolkata)**; first **INS Nilgiri** (January 2025); the seventh, Vindhyagiri, is at GRSE; indigenous content **~75%**, 200+ MSMEs
  [2.1] Next | **Project 17B** – 7 next-generation frigates
  [2.2] **INS Vikrant** | **1,000 aircraft landings**; built at **Cochin Shipyard**; commissioned 2 September 2022; ~45,000 t; MiG-29K
  [2.3] **Large Cavitation Tunnel** | ~230 m, at **NSTL Visakhapatnam** – construction inaugurated by **Rajnath Singh**
  [2.4] **Port of Refuge** | APSEZ inaugurated **India's first Port of Refuge** for vessels in distress at **Dighi (west coast)** and **Gopalpur (east coast)**; tripartite MoU with **SMIT Salvage (Royal Boskalis)** and MERC
[3] h: Chiefs and establishments
[4] table: Body | Key detail
  [4.0] Coast Guard | Est. 1978; DG **Paramesh Sivamani**
  [4.1] GSL | Est. 1957, Vasco da Gama
  [4.2] GRSE | Est. 1884, Kolkata
  [4.3] MDL | Est. 1934
  [4.4] Navy | Chief **Admiral Dinesh K. Tripathi**
  [4.5] IAF (est. 1932) | Chief **Air Chief Marshal Amar Preet Singh**
  [4.6] Assam Rifles | DG Lt Gen Vikas Lakhera
